 # Limelight and Butine — Java port
 
-This folder brings the Limelight and Butine functionality from `StudioProjects/butine test` (formerly Ma Douce) into F.R.O.G. Competition TeleOp and autonomous now service it through `CompetitionWorkers`; authored routes retain their existing behavior until an explicit collection action is added.
+This folder brings the Limelight and Butine functionality from `StudioProjects/butine test` (formerly Ma Douce) into F.R.O.G. **The optional integration is disconnected from active competition autonomous and TeleOp.** The code remains available for later deliberate use; competition currently follows its authored paths without constructing camera/planner workers or using dynamic collection, pose corrections or parking.
 
 ## Competition lifecycle integration
 
-Both competition bases construct/configure the required workers during INIT, start them before optional control-thread affinity, and service their publications after each localization update. `CompConfig.VISION_ENABLED` enables the optional vision worker by default; missing camera initialization does not disable manual TeleOp or static autonomous. Override `vision_enabled()` for an OpMode that needs no vision. `CompConfig.CONTROL_CORE = -1` leaves the control thread's eligible mask unchanged; a selected zero-based CPU is applied once during INIT, verified, and its original mask is restored during cleanup.
+`CompConfig.OPTIONAL_ASSISTANCE_ENABLED = false` gates worker construction, configuration, startup and optional autonomous queues. `CompConfig.VISION_ENABLED = false` independently disables camera startup. No active competition subclass overrides the integration gate. Disabled queue calls add no actions, and active autonomous retains its original authored parking tails, including the export that intentionally finishes holding the garden load without parking. The optional SDK timer reset, dynamic-parking setup and preemption are also skipped. TeleOp does not invoke its optional video hook.
 
-An autonomous `queue_collection(depot, params, speedScale)` action opts that OpMode into the planner worker and its required vision worker. The depot is already in the actual field frame; `params.remainingS` counts the match allowance from autonomous START, with elapsed time deducted when the action begins. An accepted collection action runs intake and lowers the flower intake, continues servicing the common loop while planning/collecting, then stops intake and raises it. Scoring remains a separately authored action. Existing routes have not been given collection actions automatically.
+When deliberately enabled for a future test, both bases can configure workers during INIT, start them before optional control-thread affinity, and service publications after localization updates. `CompConfig.CONTROL_CORE = -1` currently leaves the control thread's eligible mask unchanged; a deliberately selected zero-based CPU is applied once during INIT, verified, and its original mask is restored during cleanup. This generic control-thread utility does not start camera or planner work.
 
-TeleOp can override `planning_enabled()` and explicitly call `request_collection(...)` or `request_relocalization()`; no new driver buttons are assigned. Main preserves assistance ownership while sticks are neutral, and cancels it before manual input, aim, pose reset or alliance changes take over. Startup alone never moves the robot or requests a correction. Use `configure_workers(CompetitionWorkers workers)` to set camera/map calibration and worker core preferences before owners start. Caller code treats published planner arrays as read-only.
+With the integration enabled, an autonomous `queue_collection(depot, params, speedScale)` action requests the planner worker and its required vision worker. The depot is already in the actual field frame; `params.remainingS` counts the match allowance from autonomous START, with elapsed time deducted when the action begins. An accepted collection action runs intake and lowers the flower intake, continues servicing the common loop while planning/collecting, then stops intake and raises it. Scoring remains a separately authored action. Existing competition routes contain no active collection requests.
+
+In optional autonomous operation, elapsed time comes from the FTC SDK `getRuntime()`, reset once after START. It is copied into each planner request, including replans. `greedyAtS = 25` switches the physical planner to one-run greedy at 25 elapsed autonomous seconds. Before that, the default two-run horizon compares alternative first runs using one advisory second run. Only the first is driven; the second is discarded before the next fresh solve. No third physical run is published.
+
+The optional `queue_park()` action is available but disconnected from competition routes. A normal optional terminal park waits until the estimated live-position approach plus a one-second reserve needs the remaining AUTO time, and can interrupt an active optional action. A failed collection instead requests immediate terminal parking, skips the dependent continuation and checks that the approach fits the remaining allowance. Both paths park at most once, disable intake/shooting, preserve carried pollen and use the footprint/obstacle router. The SDK timer supplies the 30-second deadline. Collection allowance reserves terminal parking cost once. Invalid localization, a blocked approach or insufficient time stops the sequence; no unchecked direct path is substituted.
+
+Approved collection-outcome handling uses an immutable result for each attempt. `COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT` is the only collection outcome that permits its authored continuation. A fresh empty map, an infeasible run, refusal, abort or local timeout skips collection-dependent shots and paths and considers the checked parking fallback. Missing/stale vision is distinguished from an empty fresh map. STOP, the hard autonomous deadline, invalid localization and explicit manual override do not begin fallback motion. Cleanup preserves the result and cancelled planner publications cannot resume collection. See the [case-by-case outcome policy](../../../../../../../../../Butine-Collection-Outcome-Plan-2026-10-28.md).
+
+Nominal loading zones are 23 by 11 inches at Red A5 and Blue F2, with partial body overlap sufficient. `ButineParking` calculates provisional 18-inch-body targets `(19,108,0)` and `(125,36,pi)`, with one inch of overlap. Override `parking_pose()` in actual field coordinates to allocate space with the alliance partner; `field_pose(redPose)` mirrors a manual red target once. `parking_parameters()` supplies the provisional robot envelope and motion estimates. These are software defaults for the unbuilt robot, not physical calibration results. See the [planning and parking notes](../../../../../../../../../Butine-Planning-Parking-2026-10-28.md).
+
+After deliberate integration enablement, TeleOp can override `planning_enabled()` and explicitly call `request_collection(...)` or `request_relocalization()`; no new driver buttons are assigned. The retained ownership hooks preserve assistance while sticks are neutral and cancel it before manual input, aim, pose reset or alliance changes take over. They have no assistance owner in current competition operation. Startup alone never moves the robot or requests a correction. Use `configure_workers(CompetitionWorkers workers)` to set camera/map calibration and worker core preferences before owners start. Caller code treats published planner arrays as read-only.
 
 The important gamepad/Pedro/shooter/intake loop remains a single thread. No per-subsystem scheduler or compute-time budget was introduced. Cleanup cancels work, attempts independent safe hardware outputs, waits bounded intervals for original worker owners, and finally restores control affinity even when another cleanup step fails. The robot has not been built; camera calibration, physical assignment, pickup behavior and performance remain unverified.

+    static List<Candidate> plan_candidates(List<Pollen> all, VisionPose start, VisionPose depot,
+                                          Butine.Params params, double budgetS) {
+        ArrayList<Candidate> candidates = new ArrayList<>();
+        Candidate greedy = plan_run(all, start, depot, params, budgetS);
+        if (greedy != null) offer_candidate(candidates, greedy);
+        heading_candidates(all, start, depot, params, budgetS, start, candidates);
+        if (start.distance_to(depot) <= EPSILON && params.kinematics.omegaMaxRadS > EPSILON) {
+            for (double heading : new double[]{0, Math.PI / 2, Math.PI, -Math.PI / 2}) {
+                double difference = Math.abs(Butine.wrap_pi(Butine.wrap_pi(heading) - Butine.wrap_pi(start.heading)));
+                if (difference <= EPSILON) continue;
+                double alignment = difference > params.kinematics.turnDeadbandRad
+                        ? difference / params.kinematics.omegaMaxRadS + params.kinematics.turnSettleS : 0;

+                        params, budgetS - alignment, start, candidates);

+        }
+        candidates.sort(ButineIntake::compare_candidates);
+        return candidates;
+    }

+        }

+                                            Butine.Params params, double budgetS, VisionPose originalStart,

+        if (budgetS <= 0) return;

+        ArrayList<Prefix> beam = new ArrayList<>();
+        beam.add(new Prefix(origin, null));
+        int capacity = Math.min(Butine.POSSESSION_LIMIT, Math.max(1, params.capacity));
+        for (int depth = 0; depth < capacity && !beam.isEmpty(); depth++) {
+            ArrayList<Prefix> next = new ArrayList<>();
+            for (Prefix prefix : beam) {

+                    // with_alignment includes the initial turn; restore the complete allowance here.
+                    double alignment = Math.abs(Butine.wrap_pi(originalStart.heading - start.heading));
+                    double totalBudget = budgetS + (alignment > params.kinematics.turnDeadbandRad
+                            ? alignment / params.kinematics.omegaMaxRadS + params.kinematics.turnSettleS : 0);
+                    if (candidate.route.timeS + Math.max(0, params.scoreOverheadS) > totalBudget + EPSILON) continue;
+                    offer_candidate(candidates, candidate);

+            }

+            if (next.size() > LOOKAHEAD_BEAM_WIDTH) next.subList(LOOKAHEAD_BEAM_WIDTH, next.size()).clear();
+            beam = next;
+        }
+    }

+    private static Candidate with_alignment(Candidate candidate, VisionPose start, VisionPose depot,

+        ArrayList<VisionPose> points = new ArrayList<>();
+        points.add(start);
+        for (int i = 0; i < candidate.route.waypoint_count(); i++) points.add(candidate.route.waypoint(i));
+        boolean[] active = new boolean[candidate.route.segment_count() + 1];
+        for (int i = 1; i < active.length; i++) active[i] = candidate.route.intake_active(i - 1);
+        ArrayList<Contact> contacts = new ArrayList<>();
+        for (int i = 0; i < candidate.ids.length; i++) contacts.add(new Contact(
+                new Pollen(candidate.ids[i], candidate.poses[i], 1, 1, 0),
+                candidate.route.pickup_segment(i) + 1, candidate.route.pickup_fraction(i)));
+        return new Candidate(new Route(points, active, contacts, params, heading, depot), contacts);
+    }

+    private static int compare_candidates(Candidate a, Candidate b) {
+        int compared = Integer.compare(b.ids.length, a.ids.length);
+        if (compared != 0) return compared;
+        compared = Double.compare(a.route.timeS, b.route.timeS);
+        if (compared != 0) return compared;
+        int[] left = a.ids.clone(), right = b.ids.clone();
+        Arrays.sort(left); Arrays.sort(right);

+    private static void offer_candidate(ArrayList<Candidate> candidates, Candidate candidate) {

+                candidates.remove(i); i--;

+        }
+    }

+        return Arrays.equals(left, right);
+    }

+                                               HashSet<Integer> selected, VisionPose start, VisionPose depot,
+                                               Butine.Params params, double budgetS) {
+        ArrayList<Prefix> extensions = new ArrayList<>();

+            if (selected.contains(pollen.id) || pollen.confidence < params.minConfidence) continue;
+            VisionPose anchor = new VisionPose(pollen.position.x - params.intakeForwardOffsetIn * c,
+                    pollen.position.y - params.intakeForwardOffsetIn * s, start.heading);
+            if (params.field.is_clear(anchor)) seeds.add(pollen);

+    private void execute_parking_egress(ButineParking.Egress egress) {

+        Drive drive=new Drive();drive.continuous=true;
+        ButineRunner runner=new ButineRunner(two_targets(),drive,worker);

+        physical.remainingS=100;physical.elapsedAutoS=99;
+        require(runner.run_butine(HOME,physical,1),"physical asynchronous solve accepts the official elapsed timer");
+        physical.elapsedAutoS=1000;timer.elapsed=25;
+        await(runner);

+                "worker receives the copied 24.9-second snapshot despite later timer/parameter mutation");
+        require(drive.dispatched==0,"hypothetical second run never dispatches while accepting the plan");
+        runner.stop();
+        physical.elapsedAutoS=0;
+        require(runner.run_butine(HOME,physical,1),"next request samples the same official timer at twenty-five seconds");
+        await(runner);
+        require(timer.samples==2&&runner.last_plan().planningHorizonRuns==1&&runner.last_plan().runCount==1,
+                "official timer at exactly twenty-five switches the worker to one-run greedy");
+        require(physical.remainingS==100,"elapsed cutoff is not inferred from a thirty-second remaining allowance");
+        runner.stop();
+
+        drive=new Drive();runner=new ButineRunner(two_targets(),drive,worker);
+        timer=new AutoTimer(24.9);runner.set_auto_timer(timer);
+        require(runner.run_butine(HOME,params(),1),"legacy recovery trace starts before the elapsed cutoff");
+        await(runner);runner.update();runner.update();
+        long firstRequest=worker.stats().latestRequestId;
+        timer.elapsed=25;drive.busy=false;runner.update();
+        require(timer.samples==2&&runner.state()==ButineRunner.State.PLANNING
+                        &&worker.stats().latestRequestId>firstRequest,

+        await(runner);
+        require(runner.state()==ButineRunner.State.DRIVING&&runner.last_plan()!=null,

+        runner.stop();

      package org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight;
      
      import java.util.ArrayList;
      
      /** Pure partial-overlap parking geometry and intake-off routing from a live field pose. */
      public final class ButineParking {
          public static final double DEFAULT_OVERLAP_IN = 1.0;
          private static final double EPSILON = 1e-9;
          private static final double NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN = 144.0;
          private static final double LOADING_DEPTH_IN = 11.0;
          private static final double LOADING_WIDTH_IN = 23.0;
      
          private ButineParking() { }
      
          /**
           * Immutable actual-field rectangle, including its boundary tape. Bounds may be surveyed.
           * leftWall selects the outside approach and final heading; it does not mirror live poses.
           */
          public static final class Zone {
              public final double minX, maxX, minY, maxY;
              public final boolean leftWall;
      
              public Zone(double minX, double maxX, double minY, double maxY, boolean leftWall) {
                  if (!Double.isFinite(minX) || !Double.isFinite(maxX)
                          || !Double.isFinite(minY) || !Double.isFinite(maxY)
                          || minX >= maxX || minY >= maxY)
                      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Finite positive loading-zone rectangle required");
                  this.minX = minX;
                  this.maxX = maxX;
                  this.minY = minY;
                  this.maxY = maxY;
                  this.leftWall = leftWall;
              }
          }
      
          /**
           * Nominal Red A5 zone in the repo's 144-inch audience-oriented frame.
           * Manual 9.3 gives approximately 23 by 11 inches; setup 8.3 locates tape at tile seams.
           * These centered nominal bounds are model defaults, not surveyed official coordinates.
           */
          public static Zone red_zone() {
              double centerY = 3.0 * NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN / 4.0;
              return new Zone(0, LOADING_DEPTH_IN, centerY - LOADING_WIDTH_IN / 2.0,
                      centerY + LOADING_WIDTH_IN / 2.0, true);
          }
      
          /** Blue F2 is the 180-degree field rotation of Red A5, not a corner zone. */
          public static Zone blue_zone() {
              Zone red = red_zone();
              return new Zone(NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN - red.maxX, NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN - red.minX,
                      NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN - red.maxY, NOMINAL_FIELD_SIZE_IN - red.minY, false);
          }
      
          public static VisionPose default_target(Zone zone, ButineField field) {
              return default_target(zone, field, DEFAULT_OVERLAP_IN);
          }
      
          /**
           * Axis-aligned outside target using the configured body length and requested overlap.
           * Returns null if this default is blocked or the conservative wall envelope cannot park.
           * A caller may supply a different validated pose for alliance-partner coordination.
           */
          public static VisionPose default_target(Zone zone, ButineField field, double overlapIn) {
              if (!usable(zone, field, overlapIn)) return null;
              double x = zone.leftWall ? zone.maxX + field.robotLengthIn / 2.0 - overlapIn
                      : zone.minX - field.robotLengthIn / 2.0 + overlapIn;
              double y = zone.minY / 2.0 + zone.maxY / 2.0;
              VisionPose target = new VisionPose(x, y, zone.leftWall ? 0 : Math.PI);
              return is_parked(target, zone, field, overlapIn) ? target : null;
          }
      
          /**
           * Positive overlap of the centered, oriented physical body with the loading-zone volume.
           * All separating axes must overlap by the requested reserve, in inches. With zero reserve,
           * exact tangency is rejected. Collision/wall clearance remains independently mandatory.
           * This assumes the configured rectangle is occupied body, not a concave bounding box.
           */
          public static boolean is_parked(VisionPose pose, Zone zone, ButineField field, double overlapIn) {
              if (!usable(zone, field, overlapIn) || pose == null || !pose.is_finite()
                      || !field.is_clear(pose)) return false;
              double c = Math.cos(pose.heading), s = Math.sin(pose.heading);
              return axis_overlaps(pose, zone, field, overlapIn, 1, 0, c, s)
                      && axis_overlaps(pose, zone, field, overlapIn, 0, 1, c, s)
                      && axis_overlaps(pose, zone, field, overlapIn, c, s, c, s)
                      && axis_overlaps(pose, zone, field, overlapIn, -s, c, c, s);
          }
      
          private static boolean usable(Zone zone, ButineField field, double overlapIn) {
              return zone != null && field != null && !field.is_unrestricted()
                      && Double.isFinite(overlapIn) && overlapIn >= 0;
          }
      
          private static boolean axis_overlaps(VisionPose pose, Zone zone, ButineField field,
                                               double overlapIn, double ax, double ay, double c, double s) {
              double robotCenter = pose.x * ax + pose.y * ay;
              double robotRadius = field.robotLengthIn / 2.0 * Math.abs(ax * c + ay * s)
                      + field.robotWidthIn / 2.0 * Math.abs(-ax * s + ay * c);
              double zoneCenter = (zone.minX / 2.0 + zone.maxX / 2.0) * ax
                      + (zone.minY / 2.0 + zone.maxY / 2.0) * ay;
              double zoneRadius = (zone.maxX - zone.minX) / 2.0 * Math.abs(ax)
                      + (zone.maxY - zone.minY) / 2.0 * Math.abs(ay);
              double overlap = Math.min(robotCenter + robotRadius, zoneCenter + zoneRadius)
                      - Math.max(robotCenter - robotRadius, zoneCenter - zoneRadius);
              return Double.isFinite(overlap)
                      && (overlapIn == 0 ? overlap > EPSILON : overlap + EPSILON >= overlapIn);
          }
      
          /** Fixed-heading recovery from an already-invalid nominal pose, not normal free-floor travel. */
          public static final class Egress {
              public final VisionPose from, target;
              public final double timeS;
      
              private Egress(VisionPose from, VisionPose target, double timeS) {
                  this.from = from;
                  this.target = target;
                  this.timeS = timeS;
              }
          }
      
          /** Recovery, if needed, followed by a strict normal route. Execute the two phases separately. */
          public static final class Approach {
              public final Egress egress;
              public final ButineIntake.Route route;
              public final double timeS;
      
              private Approach(Egress egress, ButineIntake.Route route) {
                  this.egress = egress;
                  this.route = route;
                  timeS = (egress == null ? 0 : egress.timeS) + route.timeS;
              }
          }
      
          /**
           * Allows a checked, non-rotating exit from an authored pose outside the conservative envelope.
           * Existing nominal body/clearance overlap may only decrease; no new overlap is allowed.
           * This is recovery from an already-invalid model state, not a physical collision guarantee.
           * After braking at egress.target, the caller must recompute route() from its new live pose.
           */
          public static Approach approach(VisionPose from, VisionPose target, Butine.Params params) {
              if (from == null || target == null || !from.is_finite() || !target.is_finite()
                      || params == null || params.field == null || params.kinematics == null
                      || !params.field.is_clear(target)) return null;
              ButineField field = params.field;
              if (field.is_clear(from)) {
                  ButineIntake.Route normal = route(from, target, params);
                  return normal == null ? null : new Approach(null, normal);
              }
              if (field.is_unrestricted() || from.x < 0 || from.y < 0
                      || from.x > field.fieldSizeIn || from.y > field.fieldSizeIn) return null;
              try {
                  Butine.leg_time(from.heading, from, from, params.kinematics);
                  double inset = field.radiusIn + ButineField.ROUTING_OFFSET_IN;
                  double far = field.fieldSizeIn - inset;
                  if (inset > far) return null;
                  ArrayList<Double> xs = new ArrayList<>(), ys = new ArrayList<>();
                  add_coordinate(xs, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far, from.x)));
                  add_coordinate(ys, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far, from.y)));
                  for (int i = 0; i < field.box_count(); i++) {
                      ButineField.Box box = field.exclusion_box(i);
                      if (from.x < box.minX - EPSILON || from.x > box.maxX + EPSILON
                              || from.y < box.minY - EPSILON || from.y > box.maxY + EPSILON) continue;
                      add_coordinate(xs, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far,
                              box.minX - ButineField.ROUTING_OFFSET_IN)));
                      add_coordinate(xs, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far,
                              box.maxX + ButineField.ROUTING_OFFSET_IN)));
                      add_coordinate(ys, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far,
                              box.minY - ButineField.ROUTING_OFFSET_IN)));
                      add_coordinate(ys, Math.max(inset, Math.min(far,
                              box.maxY + ButineField.ROUTING_OFFSET_IN)));
                  }
                  Approach best = null;
                  for (double x : xs) for (double y : ys) {
                      VisionPose exit = new VisionPose(x, y, from.heading);
                      if (!egress_clear(from, exit, field)) continue;
                      ButineIntake.Route normal = route(exit, target, params);
                      if (normal == null) continue;
                      double seconds = Butine.straight_time(from.distance_to(exit), params.kinematics);
                      Approach candidate = new Approach(new Egress(from, exit, seconds), normal);
                      if (Double.isFinite(candidate.timeS)
                              && (best == null || candidate.timeS < best.timeS - EPSILON)) best = candidate;
                  }
                  return best;
              } catch (IllegalArgumentException invalid) {
                  return null;
              }
          }
      
          private static void add_coordinate(ArrayList<Double> values, double value) {
              for (double existing : values) if (Math.abs(existing - value) <= EPSILON) return;
              values.add(value);
          }
      
          /** Revalidate the complete constant-heading recovery segment immediately before dispatch. */
          public static boolean egress_clear(VisionPose from, VisionPose to, ButineField field) {
              if (from == null || to == null || field == null || field.is_unrestricted()
                      || !from.is_finite() || !to.is_finite() || !field.is_clear(to)
                      || Math.abs(VisionPose.wrap_pi(to.heading - from.heading)) > EPSILON
                      || from.x < 0 || from.x > field.fieldSizeIn || from.y < 0 || from.y > field.fieldSizeIn)
                  return false;
              // A normal start never receives a recovery waiver from the usual all-heading router.
              if (field.is_clear(from)) return field.segment_clear(from, to);
              double c = Math.cos(from.heading), s = Math.sin(from.heading);
              double halfX = field.robotLengthIn / 2 * Math.abs(c) + field.robotWidthIn / 2 * Math.abs(s);
              double halfY = field.robotLengthIn / 2 * Math.abs(s) + field.robotWidthIn / 2 * Math.abs(c);
              double margin = field.bodyClearanceIn + field.fieldReserveIn + field.trackingReserveIn;
              // With constant heading every wall deficit is affine, so endpoints bound the whole move.
              if (!deficit_not_worse(halfX + margin - from.x, halfX + margin - to.x)
                      || !deficit_not_worse(from.x + halfX + margin - field.fieldSizeIn,
                              to.x + halfX + margin - field.fieldSizeIn)
                      || !deficit_not_worse(halfY + margin - from.y, halfY + margin - to.y)
                      || !deficit_not_worse(from.y + halfY + margin - field.fieldSizeIn,
                              to.y + halfY + margin - field.fieldSizeIn)) return false;
              for (int i = 0; i < field.box_count(); i++) {
                  ButineField.Box box = field.physical_box(i);
                  if (!obstacle_recovery_clear(from, to, field, box, margin, c, s)) return false;
              }
              return true;
          }
      
          private static boolean deficit_not_worse(double initial, double end) {
              return Double.isFinite(initial) && Double.isFinite(end) && end <= Math.max(0, initial) + EPSILON;
          }
      
          private static boolean obstacle_recovery_clear(VisionPose from, VisionPose to, ButineField field,
                                                           ButineField.Box box, double margin, double c, double s) {
              double[][] axes = {{1, 0}, {0, 1}, {c, s}, {-s, c}};
              double bx = box.minX / 2 + box.maxX / 2, by = box.minY / 2 + box.maxY / 2;
              double hx = (box.maxX - box.minX) / 2 + margin;
              double hy = (box.maxY - box.minY) / 2 + margin;
              boolean initiallySeparated = false;
              for (double[] axis : axes) {
                  double rr = robot_radius(field, c, s, axis[0], axis[1]);
                  double br = hx * Math.abs(axis[0]) + hy * Math.abs(axis[1]);
                  double p = (from.x - bx) * axis[0] + (from.y - by) * axis[1];
                  if (projection_deficit(p, rr, br) < -EPSILON) initiallySeparated = true;
              }
              if (initiallySeparated) {
                  // Swept OBB = rectangle + translation segment. Its SAT axes add the segment normal.
                  for (double[] axis : axes)
                      if (swept_separates(from, to, field, bx, by, hx, hy, c, s, axis[0], axis[1])) return true;
                  double dx = to.x - from.x, dy = to.y - from.y, length = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
                  return length > EPSILON && swept_separates(from, to, field, bx, by, hx, hy,
                          c, s, -dy / length, dx / length);
              }
              // The overlap along each SAT axis is maximal at the smallest absolute center offset.
              // Checking that maximum prevents even a brief increase between the endpoints.
              for (double[] axis : axes) {
                  double rr = robot_radius(field, c, s, axis[0], axis[1]);
                  double br = hx * Math.abs(axis[0]) + hy * Math.abs(axis[1]);
                  double p0 = (from.x - bx) * axis[0] + (from.y - by) * axis[1];
                  double p1 = (to.x - bx) * axis[0] + (to.y - by) * axis[1];
                  double closest = p0 * p1 <= 0 ? 0 : Math.min(Math.abs(p0), Math.abs(p1));
                  if (projection_deficit(closest, rr, br) > Math.max(0, projection_deficit(p0, rr, br)) + EPSILON)
                      return false;
              }
              return true;
          }
      
          private static double robot_radius(ButineField field, double c, double s, double ax, double ay) {
              return field.robotLengthIn / 2 * Math.abs(ax * c + ay * s)
                      + field.robotWidthIn / 2 * Math.abs(-ax * s + ay * c);
          }
      
          private static double projection_deficit(double center, double radius, double otherRadius) {
              // Unlike intersection length, this detects deeper motion even when one interval contains the other.
              return radius + otherRadius - Math.abs(center);
          }
      
          private static boolean swept_separates(VisionPose from, VisionPose to, ButineField field,
                                                   double bx, double by, double hx, double hy,
                                                   double c, double s, double ax, double ay) {
              double rr = robot_radius(field, c, s, ax, ay);
              double br = hx * Math.abs(ax) + hy * Math.abs(ay);
              double p0 = (from.x - bx) * ax + (from.y - by) * ay;
              double p1 = (to.x - bx) * ax + (to.y - by) * ay;
              return Math.min(p0, p1) - rr > br + EPSILON || Math.max(p0, p1) + rr < -br - EPSILON;
          }
      
          /**
           * Safe intake-off path from the supplied live pose to a caller-validated parking target.
           * Route.timeS uses Butine's continuous travel/turn model; scoring overhead is excluded.
           * Invalid or unreachable geometry returns null, never a direct unchecked shortcut.
           */
          public static ButineIntake.Route route(VisionPose from, VisionPose target, Butine.Params params) {
              if (from == null || target == null || !from.is_finite() || !target.is_finite()
                      || params == null || params.field == null || params.kinematics == null
                      || !params.field.is_clear(from) || !params.field.is_clear(target)) return null;
              try {
                  // Reuse the planner's public validation without charging an extra travel leg.
                  Butine.leg_time(from.heading, from, from, params.kinematics);
                  double angle = Math.abs(VisionPose.wrap_pi(VisionPose.wrap_pi(target.heading)
                          - VisionPose.wrap_pi(from.heading)));
                  if (angle > params.kinematics.turnDeadbandRad
                          && params.kinematics.omegaMaxRadS <= EPSILON) return null;
                  ButineIntake.Route route = ButineIntake.return_route(from, target, params);
                  return route != null && Double.isFinite(route.timeS) && route.timeS >= 0 ? route : null;
              } catch (IllegalArgumentException invalid) {
                  return null;
              }
          }
      }

      package org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight;
      
      /** Hardware-free parking rules, rotated-footprint counterexamples and safe live-origin routes. */
      public final class ButineParkingChecks {
          private static int checks;
      
          public static void main(String[] args) {
              nominal_zones_and_partial_defaults();
              physical_overlap_and_rotated_false_positives();
              configured_body_and_bad_defaults();
              live_origin_detours_and_rotation();
              recovery_from_authored_nominal_endpoints();
              recovery_never_deepens_or_adds_overlap();
              System.out.println("Butine parking checks passed: " + checks);
          }
      
          private static void require(boolean condition, String message) {
              checks++;
              if (!condition) throw new AssertionError(message);
          }
      
          private static void near(double expected, double actual, String message) {
              require(Math.abs(expected - actual) < 1e-7, message + ": " + actual);
          }
      
          private static VisionPose pose(double x, double y, double heading) {
              return new VisionPose(x, y, heading);
          }
      
          private static void nominal_zones_and_partial_defaults() {
              ButineField field = ButineField.biobuzz();
              ButineParking.Zone red = ButineParking.red_zone(), blue = ButineParking.blue_zone();
              near(11, red.maxX - red.minX, "zone depth includes tape");
              near(23, red.maxY - red.minY, "nominal width is 23, not a complete tile");
              near(96.5, red.minY, "Red A5 is not a field corner");
              near(144 - red.maxX, blue.minX, "Blue mirrors Red X bounds");
              near(144 - red.maxY, blue.minY, "Blue mirrors Red Y bounds");
              VisionPose target = ButineParking.default_target(red, field);
              require(target != null, "nominal Red body can park while retaining all-heading clearance");
              near(19, target.x, "default leaves exactly one inch of physical body overlap");

      package org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight;
      
      import java.lang.reflect.Field;
      import java.util.List;
      
      /** Request identity and terminal evidence: idle and follower completion are never inferred success. */
      public final class RunnerOutcomeChecks {
          private static int checks;
          private static final VisionPose HOME = new VisionPose(40, 40, Math.PI / 2);
      
          private static final class Drive implements ButineRunner.Drive {
              VisionPose pose = HOME;
              boolean busy, continuous;
              int index, dispatches;
              double speed;
              ButineRunner.Leg leg;
              List<ButineRunner.Leg> route;
              public VisionPose pose() { return pose; }
              public boolean is_busy() { return busy; }
              public void follow(ButineRunner.Leg value, double scale) {
                  leg = value; busy = true; dispatches++;
              }
              public void stop() { busy = false; speed = 0; }
              public void set_pose(VisionPose value) { pose = value; }
              public boolean supports_continuous() { return continuous; }
              public void follow_run(List<ButineRunner.Leg> value, double scale) {
                  route = value; index = 0; busy = true; dispatches++;
              }
              public int path_index() { return index; }
              public double speed_in_s() { return speed; }
              public void hold_pose(VisionPose value) { pose = value; busy = false; speed = 0; }
              void arrive() { pose = leg.target; busy = false; }
          }
      
          private static Butine.Params point_params() {
              Butine.Params p = new Butine.Params();
              p.field = ButineField.unrestricted(); p.capacity = 1; p.lookaheadRuns = 1;
              p.remainingS = 100; p.scoreOverheadS = 0; p.iterationCap = 0; p.localSearchPasses = 0;
              return p;
          }

      package org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.core.control;
      
      import java.lang.reflect.Field;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.Butine;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.ButineField;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.ButineRunner;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.Limelight;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.PollenMap;
      import org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.limelight.VisionPose;
      
      /** Typed facade evidence and arbitration without constructing camera hardware or faking runner state. */
      public final class CompetitionCollectionOutcomeChecks {
          private static final VisionPose START = new VisionPose(20, 20, 0);
          private static int checks;
      
          public static void main(String[] args) throws Exception {
              disabled_and_preflight_requests_are_terminal();
              unavailable_and_invalid_localization_are_distinct();
              busy_refusal_keeps_the_active_attempt();
              external_finish_preserves_its_cause();
              shutdown_records_stop_without_motor_commands();
              System.out.println("Competition collection outcome checks passed: " + checks);
          }
      
          private static void disabled_and_preflight_requests_are_terminal() {
              CompetitionWorkers workers = new CompetitionWorkers(null, null, false, false);
              require(workers.collection_attempt_id() == 0 && workers.collection_result() == null,
                      "an unrequested collection has no invented result");
              require(!workers.request_collection(START, parameters(), 1), "disabled planner refuses collection");
              ButineRunner.Result refused = workers.collection_result();
              outcome(refused, ButineRunner.Outcome.REFUSED, ButineRunner.Reason.PLANNER_UNAVAILABLE);
              require(refused.attemptId == workers.collection_attempt_id() && !refused.success(),
                      "disabled refusal has matching identity and cannot authorize a shot");
              long expired = workers.decline_collection(START, ButineRunner.Outcome.TIMED_OUT,
                      ButineRunner.Reason.MATCH_ALLOWANCE_EXPIRED, "no collection allowance remains");
              require(expired != refused.attemptId && expired == workers.collection_attempt_id(),
                      "preflight failure receives a new request identity");
              outcome(workers.collection_result(expired), ButineRunner.Outcome.TIMED_OUT,
                      ButineRunner.Reason.MATCH_ALLOWANCE_EXPIRED);
              require(workers.collection_result(refused.attemptId) == refused,
                      "later preflight failure does not rewrite previous immutable evidence");
              workers.finish_collection(ButineRunner.Outcome.CANCELLED, ButineRunner.Reason.STOP_REQUESTED, "cleanup");
              workers.cancel_drive(); workers.request_stop(); workers.stop();
              outcome(workers.collection_result(), ButineRunner.Outcome.TIMED_OUT,
                      ButineRunner.Reason.MATCH_ALLOWANCE_EXPIRED);
              try {
                  workers.decline_collection(START, ButineRunner.Outcome.COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT,
                          ButineRunner.Reason.NONE, "invalid success");
                  throw new AssertionError("preflight reported success");
              } catch (IllegalArgumentException expected) { checks++; }

- path: tools\partner-auto\README.md
  content: |
      # Partner Auto Import
      
      An offline Python application for importing a partner's Pedro `.pp` project or Java autonomous source, reviewing the extracted route and stationary actions, and exporting a local JSON planning profile. It does not modify or enable Butine, Limelight or any competition OpMode.
      
      ## Start the paste/import interface
      
      From the repository root:
      
      ```powershell
      python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py --ui
      ```
      
      On Windows, `tools/partner-auto/launch.bat` starts the interface with `--detach`: Python stays running after the launcher window closes. Launching it again reopens the existing local service. Use **Stop local importer** in the page to stop it. The command above runs in the foreground, where `Ctrl+C` stops Python; add `--detach` for the background behavior. `--no-browser` leaves opening the printed URL to you. Port 8765 is preferred so drafts survive normal restarts; if occupied, an unused port is selected. `--port NUMBER` explicitly chooses a port. Background startup logs are under `build/partner-auto/server-*.log`.
      
      The source controls accept individual `.pp`/`.java` files, a Java source folder, or text pasted into the editor. **Save pasted source** retains the editable text; **New pasted source** starts another file while keeping the previous one in the source list. **Edit** reopens any saved file. Imports, errors and retries never clear the editor. Drafts and saved sources are stored in this browser and restored on refresh, where browser storage is available. **Clear** is the explicit way to remove them. Changing source or model settings requires another import before exporting.
      
      Several complete Java files can also be pasted together. The reader splits compilation units at actual package declarations, ignoring package-like text in comments/strings, and preserves original diagnostic line references. If pasted source exactly matches this FROG checkout, missing local helpers such as `PoseMirroring` can be resolved from the project and are listed in the findings. Unfamiliar partner code is not filled with guessed FROG helpers. Choose the concrete autonomous when several are found, review the route/findings, then export the JSON profile.
      
      Wi-Fi may stay connected. No internet service or online check is used: the browser sends imports only to Python at `127.0.0.1` on this computer. A local connection error means that Python could not be reached, not that Wi-Fi needs disconnecting. Reopen `launch.bat` if needed; the source remains in the editor and draft storage. Open the app through its launcher rather than double-clicking `ui.html`, which cannot run Python by itself.
      
      Requirements: Python 3.10 or newer. `.pp` import uses only the Python standard library. Java source import additionally requires a JDK 17 or newer with `java` and `javac` available through `JAVA_HOME` or `PATH`. Android Studio users can use an installed compatible JDK. Imported Java is parsed and interpreted by supported source-analysis adapters; its robot program is never executed. The importer does not run the partner's Gradle scripts or require their hardware.
      
      ## Import from the command line
      
      ### Pedro project
      
      ```powershell
      python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py tools/butine-visualizer/examples/red-pollen-seed-20261019.pp --output build/partner-auto/red-partner.json
      ```
      
      A short editable example is included at `tools/partner-auto/examples/partner.pp`.
      
      Use `--format pp` or `--format java` to choose explicitly. Automatic format selection handles one JSON `.pp` project or a set of Java sources. Import one `.pp` project at a time; unrelated projects are not combined into a single trajectory.
      
      ### Java autonomous with dependencies
      
      List the concrete autos in our source tree:
      
      ```powershell
      python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py TeamCode/src/main/java --list-entries
      ```
      
      Select an entry and export its reviewed profile:
      
      ```powershell
      python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py TeamCode/src/main/java --entry org.firstinspires.ftc.teamcode.opmodes.auto.comp.BlueFarMidPark --output build/partner-auto/blue-far-mid.json
      ```
      
      A partner repository/folder or ZIP can be passed in place of the source folder. `--source-root PATH` adds Java dependencies outside the selected file's directory. Multiple source-file arguments are also accepted. ZIP members are read in memory rather than extracted.
      
      The included plain Java example deliberately inherits from the real FROG base. This command tests importing a leaf file plus its separate dependency root:
      
      ```powershell
      python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py tools/partner-auto/examples/ExamplePartnerAuto.java --source-root TeamCode/src/main/java --entry ExamplePartnerAuto --output build/partner-auto/example-partner.json
      ```
      
      ### Plain pasted source
      
      ```powershell
      Get-Content -Raw partner-auto.java | python tools/partner-auto/import_partner_auto.py --stdin-format java --output build/partner-auto/pasted.json
      ```
      
      The same `--stdin-format pp` option accepts `.pp` JSON. If pasted Java depends on other files, supply them as input paths or with `--source-root` rather than expecting the class name alone to reveal their contents.
      
      ## Supported extraction
      
      Pedro `.pp` support includes legacy endpoint heading fields and current 1.x atomic/compound projects; explicit sequence order and repeated references; sequence and path waits; Bezier controls and interpolating through-points; tangent, constant, linear and valid piecewise headings; and declared inch/centimeter/millimeter/meter coordinates. Compound heading rules are applied over the group's arc-length progression. A changed sequence that jumps between disconnected declared origins is reported instead of receiving an invented connector. Display shapes and pollen markers do not become robot movements.
      
      Path-level waits are retained, with a finding explaining that some Pedro versions use them as visualization pauses. The partner's actual autonomous is the authority on whether the robot performs those waits. A `.pp` file does not automatically describe shooting just because a path ends near a Hive. Only explicitly represented actions can be extracted.
      
      Java support follows source-defined inheritance, methods, constants and supported helper/action patterns. Framework adapters cover FROG's inherited queues and common Pedro and Road Runner builder/action forms. The input must include the relevant parent and helper sources. Source parsing is not a promise to understand every custom FTC framework or arbitrary runtime state machine: unsupported behavior remains an explicit finding/unknown action, including branches whose motion or duration cannot be resolved. A successful parse alone is not a complete extracted autonomous.
      
      Road Runner straight segments, turns and supported sequential/parallel actions are understood. Road Runner spline geometry and concurrent temporal/displacement markers currently remain unresolved instead of being replaced with guessed straight lines or omitted actions. Raw asynchronous Pedro dispatch also requires a recognized completion wait; an intervening action cannot be assumed to happen after path arrival.
      
      The output retains action and diagnostic source references so a route can be checked against the supplied code. Preserve unresolved branches and duration bounds during review; a guessed sensor outcome must not become an exact partner schedule.
      
      ## Body and timing model
      
      Travel and turn timing are estimated from the supplied model. The interface exposes field size, partner body dimensions, clearance, cruise speed, acceleration, turn rate, start delay, timing allowance and AUTO duration. These are provisional inputs, not measured performance of the partner.
      
      For CLI imports, `--settings settings.json` supplies a JSON object with any of these keys:
      
      ```json
      {
        "field_size_in": 144,
        "robot_length_in": 18,
        "robot_width_in": 18,
        "clearance_in": 2,
        "speed_in_s": 30,
        "acceleration_in_s2": 40,
        "turn_rate_rad_s": 3.141592653589793,
        "start_delay_s": 0,
        "timing_uncertainty_s": 0.5,
        "sample_period_s": 0.1,
        "auto_duration_s": 30
      }
      ```
      
      Source-defined waits and modeled travel are labeled separately. Unknown duration stays unbounded; it does not become zero seconds. The route preview and occupied regions are predictions for offline planning. They are not observations of where the partner is, and passing a predicted departure time does not prove a lane has cleared. Runtime cooperation remains separate work.
      
      ## Export and exit status
      
      The JSON contains the selected source/entry, normalized actions, findings, model settings, estimated timeline and swept occupancy reservations where the extracted information permits them. An incomplete profile can be saved for review and retains that incomplete status. It must not be treated as an accepted collision-free partner schedule. Unknown future motion remains visible rather than disappearing from the export.
      
      Without `--output`, CLI JSON is printed to standard output. With `--output`, the file is written and non-information findings are also printed to standard error. Exit code `0` means a complete extraction, or successful `--list-entries`; `1` means selection or unresolved/invalid extraction remains; `2` means an input/launch error. Completeness concerns extracted source, not a guarantee of physical timing or safe cooperation.
      
      ## Checks
      
      ```powershell
      python -m unittest discover -s tools/partner-auto/tests -v
      ```
      
      The checks exercise `.pp` migration, execution order and waits, inherited Java extraction, source errors, timing/occupancy behavior, command-line export and the actual local HTTP import boundary. Java checks require the JDK. No robot testing is performed by this command.
      
      Validation on 2 November 2026 passed 71 test methods, including all 18 actual FROG RED/BLUE autonomous exports. CLI imports of the repository `.pp` sample and inherited Java autos produced JSON profiles. A real headless Chrome check exercised paste, HTTP import, route/occupancy rendering and export availability. Generic default motion estimates can exceed the match duration; the tool reports this rather than claiming production timing. The test log is `build/partner-auto-tests-2026-11-02.log`.
      
      Follow-up fixes passed 80 tests, adding failed-request draft retention, edited retries without duplicate classes, refresh restoration, combined Java pastes, the exact AbstractAuto + OptimalPoses + BothFlowersNoGarden input, and detached-launch/reopen/shutdown behavior. The test log is `build/partner-auto-fixes-2026-11-02.log`. Node.js is optional for the UI interaction regression checks; the importer itself still needs only Python and, for Java source, a JDK.

- path: Butine-Collection-Outcome-Plan-2026-10-28.md
  content: |
      # Collection outcome handling — 28 October 2026
      
      Status: approved, with implementation confined to the dormant optional collection code. Butine and Limelight remain disconnected from active competition autonomous and TeleOp programs: the master integration gate and camera startup flag are both false. This describes behavior for later deliberate use; it does not enable collection or replace the active authored routes and their existing static parking.
      
      The purpose is to decide what happens after each collection attempt. A stopped drive is not evidence of successful arrival. Sensor-full interruption stays immediate, the posecalculator and exported coordinates stay in place, and no new neural-network confidence threshold or random search behavior is introduced.
      
      ## One result per attempt
      
      Keep execution state separate from an immutable terminal result:
      
      | Outcome | Meaning |
      |---|---|
      | COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT | Collection or the sensor-full return reached the requested depot under the existing arrival gates. |
      | NO_TARGETS | A usable fresh map supplied no eligible pollen. |
      | NO_FEASIBLE_RUN | Observed targets exist, but no checked collection and return fit the geometry and allowance. |
      | REFUSED | The attempt never started: unavailable service, unusable vision, occupied drive or another admission failure. |
      | ABORTED | Accepted work failed, with a separate reason such as invalid localization or unsafe progress. |
      | TIMED_OUT | An action allowance or arrival wait expired; distinguish this from the hard match deadline. |
      | CANCELLED | Manual override, intentional terminal parking, STOP or shutdown ended accepted work. |
      
      Record the attempt ID, requested depot, final valid pose when available, contact count, full-sensor flag and readable reason. A refusal must not overwrite another active attempt. Cleanup preserves the terminal result; a new request cannot inherit an earlier success.
      
      ## Default decisions for each case
      
      **Successful depot arrival.** The caller may continue with its authored action whose origin and purpose match that depot. Arrival proves location, not retained inventory. Existing load and shooter-readiness conditions still apply; the collection result does not introduce a new inventory estimator or independently command a shot. A failed return with a partial or uncertain load follows the conservative parking policy below.
      
      **Full sensor during collection.** Disable intake immediately, cancel the remaining collection path and execute the existing checked return. Repeated full signals leave that return alone. If the return reaches the depot, publish COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT with the full-sensor flag, even when vision counted zero contacts. Do not invent pickup IDs. If the return fails, report that failure and apply the fallback rules below.
      
      **A fresh empty map.** Publish NO_TARGETS, finish the collection sequence, skip its dependent shot and move to terminal parking if possible. Do not drive around looking for pollen, repeatedly solve the same empty map or add a confidence cutoff to manufacture a target. Existing carried pollen remains carried.
      
      **Targets, but no feasible run.** Publish NO_FEASIBLE_RUN. Do not dispatch a partial unchecked route or repeatedly retry unchanged geometry. Skip the collection-dependent continuation and consider terminal parking.
      
      **Vision or planner unavailable.** Publish a refusal, or an abort if accepted work already began. Missing/stale evidence differs from a fresh empty map. With valid current odometry, parking can still use the static field geometry; it need not wait for vision to become ready again. A refusal cannot steal manual drive ownership.
      
      **Partial collection, interruption or failed return.** Stop collection and feeding, preserve carried pollen and report the actual outcome. Path-idle, geometric contacts and a previous attempt's success cannot establish depot arrival. The conservative fallback is parking with the load rather than an uncertain shot.
      
      **Invalid localization.** Stop. Do not park from an old cached pose or a guessed origin. A plausible target or previously clear route does not repair invalid current localization.
      
      **No checked route to park.** Stop outputs and retain the failure detail. Never substitute a direct line through an obstacle or use unrestricted geometry to force acceptance.
      
      
      [read_files: showing lines 1-40 of 62. Use code_search to locate the part you need and read a window around it, or call read_files again with offset=41 to continue.]
- path: Butine-Planning-Parking-2026-10-28.md
  content: |
      # Butine planning and terminal parking — 28 October 2026
      
      Optional software behavior for the future robot. **Butine and Limelight are disconnected from active competition autonomous and TeleOp.** `CompConfig.OPTIONAL_ASSISTANCE_ENABLED` and `CompConfig.VISION_ENABLED` are both false. Actual competition routes retain their original authored paths and static parking tails; the garden-carry export still intentionally ends without a park. No camera/planner worker, pose correction, dynamic parking or optional timer reset runs in those modes.
      
      The following planning and parking behavior remains available behind that gate for later deliberate use. The footprint, motion and intake values remain provisional. Hardware calibration and posecalculator restructuring are outside this change.
      
      ## One current run and one advisory run
      
      The physical sweep planner now evaluates alternative first runs against one second run on the remaining pollen. It compares total feasible pickups, then first-run pickups, then total predicted time. Both runs include their collection, checked depot return and scoring allowance.
      
      The plan contains at most two runs. The runner still dispatches only the first. After the real run and scoring, the caller must solve again from fresh observations; the advisory route is never dispatched automatically. The original greedy rollout remains a candidate.
      
      The finite candidate search keeps obstacle, swept-intake, incidental-contact, capacity and overshoot checks. A desktop regression demonstrates a different first run producing eight predicted contacts over two runs where the previous greedy rollout produced seven, within the same allowance. This is an algorithm example, not measured collection performance.
      
      ## Greedy cutoff and official timer
      
      Butine.Params.greedyAtS defaults to 25.0 elapsed autonomous seconds. Before the cutoff, the default horizon is two total runs. At or beyond the cutoff, the physical planner uses one-run greedy. Explicit one-run requests remain one-run; setting the cutoff to zero selects greedy immediately.
      
      When optional assistance is enabled, General uses the FTC SDK resetRuntime() once after START and getRuntime() thereafter. Active static competition operation skips this optional reset and timing policy. The previous separate autoStartedNanos match timer was removed. CompetitionWorkers supplies the SDK timer to ButineRunner; each initial request or replan samples it on the control thread and copies elapsedAutoS into the worker request. The pure planner creates no autonomous clock and does not derive elapsed time from remainingS.
      
      ## Terminal parking
      
      The optional queue_park() action provides one terminal park. Its calls are commented out in the competition exports, which keep their original authored tails. With the integration gate disabled, queue_park() and queue_collection() add no actions or parking setup. Optional parking preserves carry-into-TeleOp behavior: it adds no shot. Its alliance zone is selected in actual field coordinates.
      
      A normal optional terminal action waits until the routed parking time plus a one-second allowance needs the remaining 30-second AUTO time. An active optional action may be interrupted when parking becomes due. A failed optional collection instead requests immediate terminal parking and skips its dependent shots and paths. Intake and shooting stop, the flower intake rises, and parking starts from the current localized pose only when its checked approach fits the remaining official allowance. STOP, invalid localization, explicit manual override and the SDK 30-second deadline do not begin fallback motion.
      
      An agreed parking target is a real target: already overlapping another part of the loading zone does not skip a requested move to the partner-coordinated pose. Completion requires target proximity, heading, low measured speed and actual body overlap.
      
      Optional collection requests reserve the depot-to-park allowance once, outside the two-run collection budget. The final route is recalculated from the live position, including after an interrupted collection. A blocked approach or insufficient remaining time stops the sequence. Parking is attempted at most once and never changes a failed collection result into depot success.
      
      ## Nominal target and manual override
      
      The official loading volume is approximately 23 inches along the wall by 11 inches into the field and includes the tape. PARK requires partial robot overlap; no minimum majority or center inclusion is required. Red is at A5 and Blue at F2, not the field corners. Sources: [manual 9.3 and 10.5.4](https://ftc-resources.firstinspires.org/ftc/game/cm-html), [setup guide 8.3](https://ftc-resources.firstinspires.org/ftc/field/eventfieldguide).
      
      In the repo's nominal 0–144-inch frame, the derived Red rectangle is X=[0,11], Y=[96.5,119.5]; Blue is X=[133,144], Y=[24.5,47.5]. These are configurable model bounds derived from the official dimensions and tile placement, not a published survey.
      
      For the provisional centered 18-inch square, calculated targets are:
      
      | Alliance | X | Y | Heading | Nominal body overlap |
      |---|---:|---:|---:|---|
      | Red | 19 | 108 | 0° | 1 inch into the loading volume |
      | Blue | 125 | 36 | 180° | 1 inch into the loading volume |
      
      For later optional operation, override parking_pose() in an auto to choose an actual-field pose with your partner. To author one RED target for both alliances, return field_pose(new Pose(redX, redY, headingRadians)). The default is generated by ButineParking.default_target(). parking_parameters() controls the provisional body and motion model. These hooks do not replace any current competition route while assistance is disabled.
      
      
      [read_files: showing lines 1-45 of 71. Use code_search to locate the part you need and read a window around it, or call read_files again with offset=46 to continue.]
- path: Butine-Partner-Hive-Design-2026-11-01.md
  content: |
      # Butine: alliance cooperation and changing Hive targets
      
      1 November 2026, revised 2 November after the owner's selection of the Python approach. The offline importer is implemented under [tools/partner-auto](tools/partner-auto/README.md); runtime cooperation remains a design proposal. Butine and Limelight remain disconnected from competition operation. The robot does not exist yet; this work does not add a hardware-calibration task or change neural pollen acceptance, mapping policy or ownership of pose calculations.
      
      ## Recommended direction
      
      Keep the reliable authored opening. Use Butine for scattered pollen and remaining opportunities in space the alliance partner has agreed to leave available. The next valuable improvement is a complete feasible collection-and-score opportunity: reachable pollen, clear partner traffic, a usable shooting approach, an available shooting turn, and enough time to park.
      
      The selected implementation is a local Python paste/import application that reads Pedro .pp projects and Java source and produces partner corridors, shooting occupancy and timing ranges. Its bundled JDK bridge parses Java syntax without running imported robot code; the Python reader interprets supported inherited source/action patterns. Missing dependencies and unsupported behavior remain explicit. A common profile enables later partner-aware planning without requiring a partner to rewrite their auto.
      
      The partner's route file is useful prior information. It does not establish their actual position. Coordinate shared passages and shooting positions. An unobservable in-flight tip race is an accepted limitation, not an additional prediction project.
      
      ## What the current source actually supplies
      
      | Existing part | Consequence for the design |
      |---|---|
      | One driven run plus one advisory run, with the SDK elapsed-AUTO greedy cutoff | Retain this. More lookahead depth is not the current priority. |
      | Swept intake, incidental-contact capacity, static footprint routing, full-sensor return and typed outcomes | Retain these rather than building a new collector. |
      | One fixed depot passed to Butine.solve_sweep, including the advisory second run | A changing Cell can invalidate the assumed scoring destination. Future return choices must account for both scoring approaches. |
      | ButineIntake.Route contains segment duration and endpoint-speed information | There is a starting point for checking occupancy over time. |
      | ButineField contains static obstacles; execution checks static corridors | No partner occupancy prediction or observation currently exists. |
      | Candidate pruning keeps one preferred route per pickup-ID set | A slower corridor or delayed departure can be lost before a partner check sees it. |
      | The motion model starts and ends at zero speed | A fresh plan cannot simply be spliced into moving execution using its current timing assumptions. |
      | Shooter.nearest_hive chooses top/bottom by robotY > 72 | These are field-side names, not an observed upward-Cell decision. |
      | HivePoseEstimator reconstructs camera/world orientation internally | Reuse this calculation to derive Cell state; do not move the pose calculation elsewhere. |
      
      Relevant source: Butine.java solve_sweep; ButineIntake.java offer_candidate and motion; Shooter.java nearest_hive; HivePoseEstimator.java estimate.
      
      ## Selecting the correct Cell
      
      The official manual describes one two-Cell pivoting Hive per alliance. The permitted target is its upward-facing Cell. During a tip, comparing two center heights alone is insufficient. See [TU07, sections 9.6, 10.5.1 and G417](https://ftc-resources.firstinspires.org/ftc/game/cm-html/BIOBUZZ%20Competition%20Manual%20-%20TU07.htm).
      
      Extend the existing calculator's output with the identified alliance/Cell, opening geometry or upward-facing state, and observation timestamp. Its internal world-camera rotation and measured camera-tag rotation already give the world-tag rotation. Known rigid geometry can then describe both Cells from one usable tag observation.
      
      Use explicit identities such as audience Cell and far Cell, plus a separate state: UNKNOWN, AUDIENCE_UP, TIPPING, FAR_UP. This avoids confusing field north/south, tag identity and current physical orientation. Tag ID identifies the attached Cell; it does not by itself prove that Cell is upward.
      
      HivePoseEstimator.Result.z is estimated robot-reference height, and axisErrorDeg checks hinge-axis consistency. Neither is a Hive tilt measurement. The useful rotation exists inside the calculator but the present public result does not expose the needed scoring state. The estimator is also currently isolated from the production camera snapshot.
      
      Stability is a feeding condition, not a prerequisite for travelling to the next shooting position. When a tip from Cell A toward Cell B is recognized, immediately plan toward B's future shooting position. Use the last stable side and observed tip direction to select the expected endpoint. Wait at that position until the Hive settles and our shooting turn begins. If the partner occupies the shared shooting area, wait at the agreed approach holding point rather than entering their space. If the observed motion reverses, revise the destination using the same checked routing. This uses the existing posecalculator; it introduces no pollen-confidence filter.
      
      Treat the destination selected when leaving to collect as provisional. Recheck before committing to the final scoring approach and before feeding. If it changed, route to the other valid approach, wait in an agreed location, or carry the load to parking if the remaining allowance cannot support scoring. Never route across the partner's reserved space just because the other Cell became the target.
      
      The advisory second run must not assume the same scoring destination remains usable after the first shot. Initially allow for a possible relocation or conservative return allowance; eventually evaluate the alternative destination explicitly. Do not predict an exact tip from our geometric contact count: partner shots and retained inventory make that count incomplete evidence.
      
      ## Shared shooting position and turns

Two timelines, two truncation policies and a pipeline-switch reset of\nthe queued trail all had to agree for a detection to land in the map.\n\nRemove that machinery and project each detector frame with the robot pose\nsupplied when the frame is processed. Limelight now keeps one currentPose and\none framePose, so get_pollen(), pollen_frame_pose(), fresh-frame checks and\nclear_pollen() all describe the same frame. The worker no longer records or\nreplays a pose history: after potentially slow camera I/O it reads the newest\nsubmitted pose, retains its original submission timestamp for freshness, and\nonly an invalidation epoch can discard a queued frame. Pipeline changes no\nlonger truncate odometry, and record_pose/clear_pose_history disappear.\n\nSDK capture/targeting/parse latency and staleness remain available for\ndiagnostics and the existing frame-timing validity guard, so unusable frame\nmetadata is still rejected; it simply no longer rewinds the supplied pose.\nThe position test now labels the value \"Frame processing pose\".\n\nChecks updated to the new contract: CameraFrameAudit gains a delayed-result\ncase proving translation and heading come from the newest pose with latency\nmetadata preserved, LimelightWorkerAudit's slow-health and pipeline cases now\nassert the newest pose instead of buffered history, and the reflection-based\npush_pose/pose_at coverage in VisionChecks and VisionAuditChecks is replaced by\na stopped-camera lifecycle check. The threading document describes the single\nlatest-pose handoff instead of interpolation.\nEOF\n)\"",

}

The\nper-seed extension that plan_heading already performed moves into\nextend_prefix, and with_alignment rebuilds the route with the initial heading\nturn, so an alternative rollout can start at any decided heading. plan_run\nkeeps its exact previous behaviour for explicit greedy callers.\n\nAdd plan_candidates, a bounded first-run search: the original greedy rollout,\nthe current-heading expansion, an optional four-way heading alignment when the\nrobot is already at the depot, and a width-4 beam over pickup depth capped by\nthe possession limit. Candidates are deduplicated by pickup-ID set, at most\nthree alternatives are kept per contact count, and the original greedy rollout\nis retained as a baseline even when faster alternatives fill its bucket,\nbecause order of collection changes the remainder for the next run. Ties break\non contact count, predicted time, sorted IDs and collection heading, so map\ninput order cannot change the selection.\n\nButine.solve_sweep then chooses the best first run by total collected pieces,\nthen first-run pieces, then total predicted time, with exactly one advisory\nsecond run planned from the depot on the remainder. Only the first run is\npublished as executable; the plan reports planningHorizonRuns so callers know\nwhich horizon produced it. Two new Params fields carry the policy:\ngreedyAtS (default 25 elapsed autonomous seconds) collapses planning to one\nrun at the cutoff, and elapsedAutoS is an explicit caller-supplied snapshot\nthat is validated, never inferred from remainingS. A submitted lookahead of\nthree is clamped to two physical runs, and the plan validates both the cutoff\nand the snapshot as finite and non-negative.\n\nButineIntakeChecks adds a fixture where the greedy rollout predicts seven\ncontacts across two runs while a different first run predicts all eight within\nthe same allowance, and asserts determinism, field clearance, capacity,\nper-run depot endpoints, the exact cutoff boundary, invalid-setting rejection\nand that lookahead alternatives cannot bypass fifth-contact or blocked\novershoot rejection.\nEOF\n)\"",

  "command": "git add -- \"TeamCode/src/main/java/org/firstinspires/ftc/teamcode/limelight/ButineRunner.java\" \"TeamCode/src/main/java/org/firstinspires/ftc/teamcode/core/control/CompetitionWorkers.java\" \"tests/java/org/firstinspires/ftc/teamcode/limelight/RunnerOutcomeChecks.java\" \"tests/java/org/firstinspires/ftc/teamcode/core/control/CompetitionCollectionOutcomeChecks.java\" \"tests/java/org/firstinspires/ftc/teamcode/limelight/AsyncRunnerChecks.java\" \"tools/limelight-audit/fixtures/CompetitionWorkersAudit.java\" \"Butine-Collection-Outcome-Plan-2026-10-28.md\" && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nReport a typed terminal outcome for every collection attempt\n\nCallers could only observe state() and an idle drive, which cannot distinguish\n\"arrived at the depot\", \"empty map\", \"refused because the drive was busy\",\n\"timed out\" or \"canceled by STOP\", and a refusal could silently overwrite an\nearlier attempt's evidence. Deciding whether a queued shot or path was still\nvalid therefore meant guessing from indirect signals.\n\nIntroduce an immutable Result (attemptId, Outcome, Reason, depot, final pose,\ncontact count, full-sensor flag, detail) and seven outcomes: COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT,\nNO_TARGETS, NO_FEASIBLE_RUN, REFUSED, ABORTED, TIMED_OUT and CANCELLED.\nOnly the runner's existing arrival gates may report COMPLETE_AT_DEPOT;\nfinish(), request_stop() and the static unstarted_result() factory reject a\ncaller-supplied success, and unstarted attempts share the runner's attempt-ID\nnamespace so the facade and the runner cannot invent colliding IDs.\n\nEvery refusal and abort inside the runner now carries a cause: busy drive,\nplanner unavailable, invalid input or pose, blocked route, changed plan,\nplanner error, expired allowance, follower timeout, never-busy, pickup\nfailure, unsafe progress, manual override, pose reset, STOP or service\nfailure. Accepted work records its result before stopping motors, cleanup does\nnot rewrite a terminal result, and a new request cannot inherit an earlier\nsuccess. solve_from() distinguishes a fresh map with no eligible pollen\n(NO_TARGETS) from targets that no checked run can serve (NO_FEASIBLE_RUN), and\nrecords why a recovery return was installed so a depot arrival after a missed\npickup does not become success.\n\nButineRunner also takes an optional DoubleSupplier autonomous timer supplied by\nthe caller's SDK clock. It copies the sampled elapsed time into each initial\nsolve and replan as elapsedAutoS, rejects non-finite or negative values before\nsubmitting a request, and never runs its own match clock; the pure planner\nstill does not derive elapsed time from remainingS.\n\nCompetitionWorkers keeps a bounded map of recent attempt results, exposes\ncollection_attempt_id()/collection_result(id), records preflight refusals and\nallowance expiries through decline_collection(), and converts internal aborts,\nvision loss, pose reset, service faults and shutdown into typed terminal\nevidence through finish_collection(). cancel_drive() now requires an explicit\nreason, and shutdown records STOP without issuing motor commands or erasing the\nresult.\n\nAdds RunnerOutcomeChecks and CompetitionCollectionOutcomeChecks, wires the\noutcome assertions into the standalone CompetitionWorkersAudit, and extends\nAsyncRunnerChecks with the official-timer handoff: the timer is sampled only on\nthe caller thread, a 24.9 s snapshot selects two runs while 25.0 selects one,\nreplans re-sample, and an invalid timer declines or stops without dispatching\nanother pickup. The approved policy is recorded in\nButine-Collection-Outcome-Plan-2026-10-28.md.\nEOF\n)\"",

  "timeout_seconds": 30
}

Allocating the remaining space with an\nalliance partner needs a checked, live-position path to a target that actually\noverlaps our own loading volume, plus a way to recover from the robot ending an\nautonomous where the conservative all-heading envelope says it may not be.\n\nButineParking models the nominal loading volume from the official dimensions:\n23 inches along the wall by 11 inches into the field, including tape, at Red A5\nand Blue F2 rather than the field corners. It derives the Red rectangle in the\nrepo's 0-144 inch frame and the 180-degree field-rotation for Blue, and\ncalculates a default target from the configured body length plus one inch of\noverlap, accepting it only if the pose is clear and every separating axis\noverlaps by the requested reserve. is_parked() tests the centered, oriented\nrectangle against the zone on four axes, so a rotated body that merely looks\nclose cannot pass.\n\nroute() reuses the planner's return route and motion model and never submits a\ndirect line through an obstacle. approach() adds a fallback for a robot left\noutside the conservative envelope: a non-rotating exit to the nearest\nclear coordinate derived from the field bound and exclusion boxes, revalidated\nwith a swept-rectangle check that may only decrease existing wall deficit or\nobstacle overlap and may never create new overlap. Egress and the strict normal\nroute are separate phases, so the caller re-routes from its measured arrival\nafter braking instead of assuming the planned exit was reached.\n\nAdds ButineParkingChecks, which covers the nominal rectangles and mirrored\nbounds, rotated false positives, configured body sizes, blocked defaults,\nlive-origin detours, recovery from authored endpoints, and the rule that\nrecovery never deepens or adds overlap. Also registers ButineParkingChecks and\nRunnerOutcomeChecks in the standalone audit runner. The one-current/one-\nadvisory-run and parking policy this supports, including the SDK timer and the\ncalculated 18-inch targets (19, 108, 0) for Red and (125, 36, pi) for Blue, is\nrecorded in Butine-Planning-Parking-2026-10-28.md.\nEOF\n)\"",

  "timeout_seconds": 30
